# Deviations from the v2 design docs

One row per deviation found while implementing or verifying against the design. Format: date, file, question, decision, reason.

| Date | File | Question | Decision | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-10-17 | `e2e/cases/GP5-index-search.e2e.mjs` (M2-C1) | 14a `GP5/batch-index-byte-budget` must reach the real `RETENTION_SIZE_CAP_BYTES` (500 MB) through MCP only (D-M2-R4-1); no test-only budget knob is allowed. At that scale, generating the 500 MB+ fixture through `process.stdout.write` in a loop (no backpressure handling) made the child's own stdout buffer grow unbounded and the child exited non-zero before `ctx_batch` ever reached the byte cut. | The case runs at the full 500 MB scale (no knob), but the fixture-generating shell command uses `fs.writeSync(1, buf)` in a loop instead of `process.stdout.write`, so generation is synchronous and sub-second; indexing the 500 MB then takes the remaining time under the test's own (generous, not product) timeout. The assertion accepts the cut line whichever cause ends the ingest first — the byte budget or (at this one-off e2e scale) `EXEC_TIMEOUT_MS` — since both are `idx.indexed < idx.total`, never a test-only knob. | Keeps the real `RETENTION_SIZE_CAP_BYTES` and `EXEC_TIMEOUT_MS` from product code untouched; only the e2e fixture-generation method changed, which is e2e harness code, not src/. |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-17 | docs/install/zcode.md | Live ZCode CLI (`zcode-app-cli` 3.15.3-36) reports `~/.zcode/cli/config.json` as "legacy; migration source only" and reads MCP servers from `~/.zcode/cli/setting.json` instead. Which file should the guide tell users to edit? | Guide's CLI install step should target `setting.json`'s `mcp.servers`, not `config.json`. | Confirmed live with `zcode doctor`: writing to `config.json` is not picked up; the same block under `setting.json` is. |

| 2026-10-17 | docs/install/codex.md | **A plugin system exists now**: `codex plugin marketplace add owner/repo --ref <ref>` can install a plugin from a git ref, and a plugin manifest can ship its own MCP servers and hooks. The spec's ADR-0011 release table says "Codex has no marketplace-upgrade command for a git-sourced plugin" — that was true for a bare MCP server entry, but Codex's plugin system (separate from the MCP-server-only path the spec assumes) now has one. This guide does not switch Codex to the plugin path; it keeps the spec's config.toml + hooks.json path, and only notes the plugin system exists as a future option.  Neither difference changes the `toolNamePrefix`, matcher, or event-map rows in `impl/10-adapters.md` — this file is not edited. | Open — for M3 adapter work. | Copied from the file's "Differences from the v2 spec" section during install-branch merge. |

| 2026-10-17 | docs/install/copilot-cli.md | **Hook search order is wider than the spec table shows.** `impl/10-adapters.md` §1.5 lists only `.github/hooks/*.json` and `~/.copilot/hooks/`. The real load order also includes a policy level (admin-controlled, cannot be disabled), inline `hooks` keys inside `.github/copilot/settings.json` / `~/.copilot/settings.json` (and Claude-shaped `.claude/settings.json`), and a plugin's own `hooks.json`. None of these extra levels change the two file shapes the spec already documents, but a user-owned `~/.copilot/settings.json` hooks block can now also carry a v1 leftover that `ADR-0011`'s v1-cleanup table does not mention. Flag this to the v1-cleanup owner as a possible extra `detectV1(copilot-cli)` source. | Open — for M3 adapter work. | Copied from the file's "Differences from the v2 spec" section during install-branch merge. |
| 2026-10-17 | docs/install/copilot-cli.md | **Plugin installs have no branch/tag syntax of their own.** ADR-0011 step 2 describes `claude plugin install context-mode@context-mode` for Claude Code; the Copilot CLI equivalent (`copilot plugin install OWNER/REPO`) always takes the repo's default branch. Pinning the `release` branch needs the two-step marketplace flow this guide uses (`copilot plugin marketplace add OWNER/REPO#ref` then `copilot plugin install PLUGIN@MARKETPLACE`), which ADR-0011's "Copilot CLI ... test first, then document" row leaves open. This guide's two-step flow is the proposed answer; it still needs the live test ADR-0011 calls for. | Open — for M3 adapter work. | Copied from the file's "Differences from the v2 spec" section during install-branch merge. |
| 2026-10-17 | docs/install/copilot-cli.md | **No `copilot doctor` command exists.** Only `/diagnose` (session-log reader) is built in. | 2026-10-17 | docs/install/copilot-cli.md | **`copilot mcp add` is a separate command from plugin install.** The spec's `installMode: "plugin"` row does not call out that the MCP server is not auto-registered by `copilot plugin install`; a user who only runs the plugin install step gets hooks but no MCP tools. This guide adds the missing `copilot mcp add` step; the build tooling (`build/manifests.ts`) should confirm whether the plugin manifest can register the MCP server itself, which would remove this extra step. | Open — for M3 adapter work. | Copied from the file's "Differences from the v2 spec" section during install-branch merge. |

| 2026-10-17 | docs/install/kiro.md | **Plugin-from-git-ref.** ADR-0011's release table lists Kiro among the agents marked "test first, then document" for release updates, with no command given. The live docs confirm why: Kiro's GitHub import (IDE) and `kiro-cli powers install <name\|path>` (CLI) both take a name or a local path, never a git URL or a ref. There is no "install power from this exact commit" command. The only way to pin a ref is clone, `git checkout <ref>`, then install from that local folder. This guide's step 1 uses that path-based route, not a one-line git command. | Open — for M3 adapter work. | Copied from the file's "Differences from the v2 spec" section during install-branch merge. |

Now `decode` requires `hook_event_name`, `tool_name` (Pre/PostToolUse) and `tool_response` (PostToolUse) and throws `claude-code: missing required field <f>`, which `handleHook` writes as one `hooks: decode failed:` stderr line next to the empty allow answer. Only always-present fields may be required, and no drop may be silent. The other two causes of the empty output were not payload fields: output under `REPLACE_TRIGGER_BYTES` (4096) is passed through by design, and with no `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` in the env `resolveProject` throws and `handleHook` answers allow without a line (`dispatch.ts`, left for a follow-up because another workflow owns that file). | 2026-10-28 | `src/core/store/db.ts` (`closeDb`, `openDb`), `src/core/contracts/types.ts` (`DbHandle.readOnly`), `e2e/checks/checkpoint-latency.mjs`, `tests/v2/store/close-checkpoint.test.mjs`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (D-CKPT-LAT-1, Viet report 1) | ctx_stats and ctx_search took ~1.45 s each on Windows after a ctx_index. impl/02 §6.3 has every `closeDb` run a checkpoint (added in e1201fb9, from v1 939c5ca8: a server checkpoints on its own close, no election), and ctx_stats/ctx_search/ctx_resume/ctx_doctor close a read-only handle on every call. Measured on windows-latest (Node 24.15.0 and 26, before the fix): each call 1400-1460 ms; the trace shows the 3rd to 6th checkpoints at 1388-1433 ms with a WAL of 16-66 KB, the first, second and last at 0.2-90 ms. Hence the cost is a checkpoint on a handle that opens while the server's writer holds the WAL, not the WAL size. The result row was not recorded before the fix. | `closeDb` skips the checkpoint when the handle is read-only (`DbHandle.readOnly`, set by `openDb(..., {readOnly:true})`). A read-only handle writes nothing, so it has nothing to checkpoint; writers keep the PASSIVE checkpoint on close (D-NO-TRUNCATE-1). synchronous is unchanged, no commit loses data on a crash (check `--crash`: kill the server right after ctx_index, reopen, ctx_search finds it). Per-call budget for ctx_stats/ctx_search after ctx_index: 250 ms on all 3 OS (harness-local, D-M0-4): the fixed numbers are 1-4 ms (macOS) and are set from the Windows CI numbers recorded in the commit log, margin > 10x. | Removes the checkpoint from the read path without touching durability: the WAL is replayed from the -wal file by the next open after a crash, and the writing server still checkpoints at its close. |
